Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

1 
2327 國巨(Yageo)

報告日期： 2026/10/30 研 究 員：洪瑞陽  (Ryan Hung) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 既有調價與 AI 產品出貨增加，帶動 3Q26 毛利率升至 41.0% 、
EPS 達 5.83 元；第四季雖為消費性淡季，公司仍指引營收、
毛利率及營益率小幅季增，呈現淡季不淡。  
 3Q26 AI 相關應用占營收 19.8% ，較前季 16% 提高 3.8 個百分
點， MLCC 、鉭電容與電源端產品共同帶動成長。第三季訂單
出貨比仍接近 2，公司規劃 2027 年 MLCC 及鉭電容擴產 10%
至 15% ，自第二季及下半年分批開出。  
 預估 2026 ／2027 年營收 1,841.63 ／2,498.39 億元 ，YoY 增長
38.5% ／35.7% ，EPS 為 20.68 ／35.71 元。 2027 年獲利成長
來自既有調價全年化、稼動率升至滿載、擴產及高階產品組合
提升。

一、 AI 需求帶動，第四季營收與獲利率續增  
3Q26 營收 495.32 億元， QoQ 增長 11.4% 、YoY 增長 49.7% ，連續
第七季季增，創下單季營收歷史新高。價格調整逐步反映，加上
AI 產品組合與營運效率改善，毛利率達 41.0% ，較前季提高 2.5
個百分點；營業利益率 29.5% ，EPS 為 5.83 元。投入成本提高抵
銷部分效益，但營收與毛利成長仍帶動獲利增加。

AI 相關應用占營收已由 1Q26 的 15%、2Q26 的 16%， 提高至3Q26
的 19.8% 。按公布占比計算，第三季 AI 相關營收約 98.07 億元，
較第二季約 71.13 億元增長 37.9% 。成長涵蓋 MLCC 、鉭電容，以
及電源端使用的薄膜與鋁電容，反映運算、通訊與供電需求共同
增加。工業營收亦已連續七季成長，車用維持正向，消費性營收
仍有成長，但速度低於其他應用。

第四季通常為消費性產品淡季，公司考量接單及在手訂單仍強，
指引營收、毛利率與營業利益率均較第三季小幅提高。本研究採
稼動率約 95% ，以既有調價遞延反映支撐淡季營收，預估 4Q26
營收 520.09 億元， QoQ 增長 5.0% 、YoY 增長 44.6% ，毛利率
42.78% 、營業利益率 31.43% 、EPS 為 6.36 元。

二、稼動率接近滿載，新增產能 2Q27 起貢獻  
3Q26 標準型與利基型產品稼動率均接近 95% ，公司預期 4Q26 超
過 95% ，並透過去瓶頸、效率改善與逐步擴充產能支應訂單。第
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：892 元 
      25X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：543 元 
     15X 2027 EPS 
     2026/10/29 收盤價

2 
三季營收已較前季增加 11.4% ，訂單出貨比仍接近 2，顯示較高出
貨基期下接單維持高水準 ， 尤其MLCC 與鉭電容等 AI 相關產品需
求較強。

公司規劃 2027 年 MLCC 及鉭電容產能較目前增加 10% 至 15% ，
部分於第二季、部分於下半年開出。此為相對目前產能的整體增
幅，新增產能須隨設備交付、驗證與量產進度反映；公司亦表示，
擴產後希望稼動率回到 85% 至 90% 的健康水準。

三、漲價分批反映， 2027 年獲利仍以電容產品為主  
國巨 2026 年 2 月調高晶片電阻價格 10% 至 20%， 並同步調整鉭電
容； 7 月調價範圍擴至各產品線，假設幅度約 15% 至 25% ，並涵
蓋 EMS 與 OEM 直接客戶，通常延後一至兩季反映，因此第三季
已認列部分效益， 10 月新一季價格生效。

MLCC 與鉭電容為目前成長最明確的產品。第三季 MLCC 占營收
21.5% ，高於前季 19.1% ；鉭電容占 23.7% ，營收季增 10.0% 、年
增 45.3%。 公司表示鉭粉價格自年初趨於穩定 ， 已進行策略採購及
長期供應鏈布局，支持後續供貨與成本管理。

四、預估 2026 ／2027 年 EPS 為 20.68 ／35.71 元 
預估 2026 ／2027 年營收 1,841.63 ／2,498.39 億元 ，YoY 增長 38.5%
／35.7% ，EPS 為 20.68 ／35.71 元。2027 年隨既有調價全年反映、
假設稼動率提高至 100%，以 及MLCC 與鉭電容新增產能自第二季
起分批貢獻，帶動營收與獲利成長。

五、投資建議：買進，目標價 892 元 
參考國外 MLCC 大廠本益比，以及國巨 AI 佔比持續提高，給予
25 倍本益比，以 2027 年 EPS 計算 35.71 ，目標價 892 元，投資建
議為買進。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況 
一、完整被動元件產品線， AI 相關由板端延伸至電源  
國巨透過產品開發及 Pulse 、KEMET 、奇力新等併購布局，產品涵
蓋 MLCC 、鉭電容、鋁電容、薄膜電容、電阻、磁性元件與感測
器，服務工業、運算、通訊、車用及消費性市場。 AI 設備從機房
供電、機櫃電源轉換到運算板，都需要處理電壓穩定、濾波、儲
能及訊號干擾；不同位置的電壓、空間與可靠度要求不同，使完
整產品組合有利於參與客戶多個供電環節。

3 
公司第三季 AI 相關應用占比接近兩成，已分布於多項產品線。隨
運算功耗提高，成長範圍由靠近 GPU 與 ASIC 的板端電容，擴大
至通訊設備、電源模組及機櫃供電。磁性元件可參與電源轉換及
濾波，電阻用於訊號與電流相關功能，感測產品則提供溫度等監
測需求。

供應能力也逐漸成為競爭條件。國巨的多產品、全球製造據點及
既有客戶關係，有助協調產品開發與後續供貨； TDK 與太陽誘電
討論先進 MLCC 、電感的共同開發及製造、採購合作，亦反映高
容值、高可靠度與穩定供應的重要性提高。

二、MLCC 與鉭電容為主軸，高容值與穩定性優勢擴大  
AI 運算功耗與供電密度增加，要求電容在有限空間內提供足夠電
容量，並維持供電穩定。 MLCC 具備小型化與高密度配置的優勢，
對高容值、小尺寸與可靠度的要求隨平台升級提高；鉭電容則具
備高容值與電容量穩定性優勢，可配合運算及通訊設備的不同低
壓電源設計，支應儲能與濾波需求。兩者實際搭配取決於電路位
置、電壓、體積與成本。

國巨透過 KEMET 具備鉭電容與多類電容產品布局，可配合客戶
選擇不同供電方案。當高容值 MLCC 供應吃緊或設計成本提高
時，其他電容可提供設計選項，但轉換仍須完成電路調整與驗證，
不能將短缺直接換算成鉭電容新增訂單。本研究以目前已觀察到
的產品成長、擴產與價格假設推估， 2027 年 MLCC 及鉭電容營收
分別為 628.36 億元及 669.79 億元， YoY 增長 65.6% 及 51.1% ，合
計營收占比由 2026 年的 44.67% 升至 51.96% 。 
圖一、各類別營收佔比

4

電源端亦提供後續產品升級機會。隨資料中心朝更高功率及 800V
直流供電方向發展，不同轉換位置將提高耐壓、溫度穩定性及效
率要求。高壓端與轉換後低壓端所需元件不同，國巨可透過陶瓷、
薄膜、鋁電容與磁性元件參與多段供電。

三、日韓接單與交期延長，高階電容供需偏緊延續  
日韓大廠的出貨與接單，顯示高階電容需求已反映在營運數據。
依， JEITA 公布最新 2026 年 7 月日廠被動元件全球出貨額 4,280
億日圓，年增 23.2% ；村田因 AI 伺服器用需求急速成長，村田製
作所將擴建 MLCC 工廠，滿足需求增加。村田之前 4 月宣布，計
畫在截至 2028 年 3 月為止的 2 年 期 間，對MLCC 生產設備投資約
800 億日圓，提升 10-15% 產能。關於投資計畫，這次中島規巨表
示，「正評估能將投資時程提前多少」 。表示「原先預估位於島根
縣出雲市的工廠產能要到 2030 年才會滿載，如今卻可能提前至
2026 年就達到滿載」

根據 Future Electronics 最新資料亦顯示交期延長 。 村田低於1μF
產品交期由 9 月 26–28 週延長至 10 月 30–36 週，高於 1μF 及
車規產品由 26–30 週延長至 30–36 週； TDK 低於 1μF 產品由
26–36 週延長至 30–40 週，高 於1μF 及車規產品由 26–48 週調
整至 30–48 週。三星電機上述三類產品均由 30 週以上調整至 52
週，價格維持向上。國巨低於 1μF、高於 1μF 及車規產品交期
均維持 30–52 週，交期與價格趨勢持續向上，整體而言， AI 伺服
器與電源規格升級持續帶動被動元件需求，交期不僅維持高檔，
主要 MLCC 廠及部分電阻產品更進一步拉長，漲價品項亦有增
加，延續供不應求的正向看法。

高效能晶片的 AI 伺服器中不可或缺的元件。隨著大型科技公司準
備明年推出下一代 AI 伺服器，三星電機預計 AI 用 MLCC 的需求
將達到今年的約八倍 ， 這項預測較上一季公佈的超七倍有所上調。

國巨本次法說表示，全球代理商庫存在過去三個月由約五個月降
至四個月，大中華區代理商維持兩個月以下。供應商接單偏強、
交期延長與通路庫存下降相互呼應，增加客戶提前確保供應的需
求。

四、滿載提高既有產出， 2027 年擴產分批支應訂單

本研究預期 2027 年高階電容供需仍偏緊，主要考量需求成長、產
品升級與新增供給之間存在時間差。 AI 平台提升功耗與供電密
度，對電容量及小型化提出更高要求；高階產品也會改變產能配
置，設備投入增加未必等比例轉化為可交付的標準品顆數。三星
規劃菲律賓新廠於 2027 年量產高附加價值 MLCC，同時仍預期高
階供應偏緊，村田將兩年擴產 10-15% 計畫考慮提前，皆是供不應
求現象。

國巨新增產能自第二季及下半年陸續開出，第一季主要仍依靠既
有產能與去瓶頸。本研究採 MLCC 、鉭電容年底有效產能較目前
增加 15% ，全年平均增幅為 7.5% 。因此，年底產能增加 15% 不等
於全年供給增加 15%； 加上第三季訂單出貨比仍接近 2， 新增供給
需先支應既有接單與後續需求持續。

基於交期延長、高階產品需求增加及擴產分批到位，本研究假設
國巨於 2027 年針對 MLCC 及鉭電容有機會再調價 ， 根據今年國巨
2026 年 2 月調高晶片電阻價格 10% 至 20% ，並同步調整鉭電容；
7 月調價範圍擴至各產品線，假設幅度約 15% 至 25% ，先假設明
年特定產品再調升 15% ，並假設於下半年完整反應。此輪假設調
價僅涵蓋兩項電容，屬同規格售價提高，與既有升規組合及 2026
年調價全年化分開計算 。(註：15% 幅度及生效時點為本研究假設，
並非公司已公布指引。 )

成本方面，公司表示鉭粉價格已趨穩定，且已進行策略採購，支
持供貨及成本管理。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

司營收小幅成長指引，預估營收季增 5%至 520.09 億元， YoY 增

二、預估 2027 年營收 2,498.39 億元， EPS 為 35.71 元 
2027 年各季維持 100% 稼動率 ，MLCC 與鉭電容有效產能自第二季
起逐步增加 ， 並沿用高階產品組合提升的假設 。 第一季營收562.55
億元， QoQ 增長 8.2% ，反映稼動率由 95% 升至 100% 、調價剩餘
效益及產品組合提升。另假設第二季僅 MLCC 及鉭電容再調價
15%，第三季起完整反映，第四季維持新價格，貢獻全年新增營收
96.39 億元。第二至第四季營收分別為 587.39 、659.50 、688.95 億
元，全 年 營 收2,498.39 億元 ，YoY 增長 35.7%。 預估毛利率47.09%、
營業費用 247 億元、費用率 9.89% ，營業利益 929.55 億元， YoY

### Page 1

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 10 月 30 日

1 群益投顧 
國巨 (2327 TT) 整體 3Q26 財報表現優異，其中營收為 495.3 億元 (QoQ +11.4% 、 YoY 
+49.7%) ，主因受惠 AI 帶 動 相 關 被 動 元 件 增 溫 ， 其 中AI 占比較 2Q26 的 16% 提升至
近 20% ； EPS 5.83 元，優於 預期 。 然 展 望 4Q26 ，受 PC 市 場 疲 軟 及 消 費 性 電 子 需 求
放緩抵銷預期 AI 於 4Q 帶 來 的 強 勁 增 長，國 巨 4Q26 營收將從預估季增雙位數下修至
微 幅 季 增，不 過 因 VR 將於 2027 年 正 式 放 量，被 動 產 能 供 需 將 會 更 緊，故 維 持 整 體 被
動 元 件 漲 價 趨 勢 向 上 ， 看 法 為 短 空 長 多 。

研 究 員 ： 葉 芷 瑄   
vanessa.yeh@cim.capital.com.tw

國巨 3Q26 法 說 會 重 點：  
國巨 (2327 TT) 於昨 (29) 舉辦 3Q26 法 說 會 ， 整 體 3Q26 財 報 表 現 優 異 ， 其 中
營收為 495.3 億元 (QoQ +11.4% 、 YoY +49.7%) ， 主 因 受 惠 AI 帶 動 相 關 被 動
元 件 增 溫 ， 其 中 AI 占比較 2Q26 的 16% 提 升 至 近 20% ； 毛 利 率 因 產 品 組 合
轉佳至 41.0%(QoQ +2.5ppts 、 YoY +4.8ppts) ； OPM 29.5%(QoQ +3.1ppts 、
YoY +6.6ppts) ； 歸 屬 母 公 司 稅 後 淨 利 為 119.8 億元 (QoQ +27.2% 、 YoY 
+88.5%) ；EPS 5.83 元 ， 優 於 群 益 預 估 的 5.15 元 。 然 展 望 4Q26 ，受 PC 市場
疲 軟 及 消 費 性 電 子 需 求 放 緩 抵 銷 預 期 AI 於 4Q 帶 來 的 強 勁 增 長 ，BB ratio
略 較 前 季 呈 現 微 幅 下 滑，國 巨 4Q26 營 收 將 從 預 估 季 增 雙 位 數 下 修 至 微 幅 季
增 ， 不 過 因 VR 將於 2027 年 正 式 放 量 ， 被 動 產 能 供 需 將 會 更 緊 ， 故 維 持 整
體 被 動 元 件 漲 價 趨 勢 向 上 ， 看 法 為 短 空 長 多 。

產業速報

2 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

公司拜訪快報

旺宏(2337)：下修 2027 年獲利展望 
建議：買進轉中立

一 、 旺宏為記憶體製造大廠 ， 主要產品為NOR 與 NAND 
Flash 
旺宏目前有一座 8 吋廠和一座 12 吋廠，主要產品分為
NAND Flash(包含 SLC NAND 以及 eMMC)、NOR Flash、
Mask ROM 與晶圓代工，NAND、NOR、ROM 與晶圓
代工分別佔 3Q26 營收比重 47%、46%、5%與 2%，為
全球最大 ROM 生產商以及 NOR Flash 領導廠商 ，2026
年資本支出 378 億元遠高於 18.61 億元。NOR FLASH
主要應用於消費性、PC、通訊、車用、工規、醫療和航
太市場 ， 旺宏於非PC 和消費性應用占比逐年增加 。ROM
主要應用於遊戲機市場，主要客戶為任天堂。晶圓代工
由 8 吋廠生產，主要產品為 MCU、IoT、電源管裡 IC 與
類比 IC 等產品。 
二、營收成長主要來自於產品漲價，3Q26 EPS 5.52 元
創新高但低於預期之 6.93 元 
3Q26 營收 242.37 億元(+27%QoQ)低於預期之 271.44
億元，主因為產能轉換影響產出較預期高，其中 NAND
和 NOR 營收分別+39%QoQ 和+21%QoQ， 受惠產品漲
價效益以及認列存貨跌價迴轉利益，毛利率由上季
64.4%提升至 70.5%，加計提列員工酬勞致使營業費用
高預期，營業利益 129.32 億元(+44%QoQ)低於預估值
20%，稅後純益 109.51 億元(+42%QoQ)，EPS 5.52 元
創新高但低於預期之 6.93 元。 
三、NOR 和 SLC NAND 價格漲勢延續 ， 估計4Q26 EPS 
6.73 元 
NOR 和 SLC NAND 價格漲勢延續，至於高價 MLC 迫
使客戶轉用 SLC 或 TLC，推估旺宏 eMMC 成長動能不
如前幾季強勁 ， 估計4Q26 營收272.8 億元(+13%QoQ)，
受惠於報價持續上漲，毛利率提升至 74.2%，在業外無
重大損益影響下，稅後淨利 133.63 億元(+22%QoQ)，
EPS 6.73 元。 
三、eMMC 現貨價回檔，將壓抑獲利想像空間 
3Q26 存貨 115.37 億元(+18%QoQ)， 主因為半成品增加，
至於 NOR 存貨評價自 3Q25 逐季貢獻利益，此增加毛
利率上檔空間。受惠於國際大廠三星停產低容量
4GB-32GB MLC NAND，致使旺宏低容量 eMMC 需求
大幅提升，由於此塊業務利潤最佳，故公司亦調配更多
產能於此，量價齊揚下致使NAND 今年營收成長性為各
產品線之最。3Q26 NAND 營收 QoQ+27%，儘管產能
調配持續增加出貨量，但 eMMC 占 NAND 營收 53%低
於上季之 58%， 推估主因在於價格漲勢不如SLC NAND，
至於 eMMC 新增產能效益需至 2H27 才能見到 ， 並以96
層 3D NAND 做為生產主力 。 根據CFM 閃存市場報價，
8GB-32GB eMMC 價格皆於 10 月轉趨疲弱，且集邦亦
認為高價 MLC 將促使客戶轉用 SLC 或 TLC，4Q26 MLC 
eMMC 價格恐將回檔 ， 在目前長約客戶營收占比極低下 ，
將壓抑旺宏明年獲利想像空間。 
就 NAND 3Q26 各應用營收表現而言，以電腦
+114%QoQ 成長性最佳，車用+19%QoQ 最差，消費性
電子、通訊、工規/醫療/航太則分別+43%QoQ、
+35%QoQ 和+41%QoQ。此外，集邦認為 MLC NAND
極度短缺將致使部分工控 、 車用客戶改採用SLC NAND，
並預期 2H26 SLC NAND 合約價較 1H26 大幅調漲
120-170%。 
四、NOR 產能資源將放在高容量 AI、太空以及醫療應
用上  
3Q26 NOR Flash 營收+21%QoQ，出貨量-12%QoQ，
主因並非需求減少，而是公司將產能分配至獲利更好的
NAND 和 eMMC 產品線，ASP 則受惠於產能排擠效應
而季增 37%，就 NOR 2Q26 各應用營收表現而言，除
車用營收+7%QoQ，電腦、消費性電子、通訊、工規/
醫療/航太皆有 QoQ 雙位數以上增幅。在NOR 2027 年
產業供需展望上，華邦透過併購 Infineon 記憶體事業取
得更多產能，旺宏將不再擴充擴充傳統應用 NOR，將資
源集中於高容量的 AI、 太空以及醫療應用上 ， 推估NOR
產業供需仍吃緊。

### Page 1

M
Idea
Chroma Ate Inc.  | Asia Pacific
2H26 Driven by Semis and
Photonics; 2027 Seen as
Another Strong Year
A unique combination of power, electrical and optical testing
tool capabilities position the company well in AI deployment.
Stay OW.
We see 3Q26 EPS of NT$13.71: Chroma's 3Q26 revenue came in at NT$17,926mn,
up 32% QoQ and 180% YoY. We believe the strength was driven mainly by the
semiconductor and photonics businesses, while the power testing business likely

remained at an elevated level. Within the semiconductor and photonics businesses,
SL T  should benefit from AI HPC customers' ramping; meanwhile, optical testing
tools for transceivers and, to a lesser extent, CPO, are also seen as growth drivers
during the quarter. We estimate GM of 60.5% (flat QoQ) as scale tailwinds should
be largely offset by a less favorable product mix, while OPM should expand by
0.5ppt QoQ to 39.3% on some operating leverage. Overall, we expect EPS of NT

$13.71 (+13% QoQ/+15% YoY).
Growth momentum seen sustained into 2027: Looking ahead, we expect Chroma
to see another year of strong growth, supported by the power testing (51% growth
for 42% mix) and semi & photonics businesses (58% growth for 40% mix).
• Power testing: The ever-increasing power consumption of AI clusters should
continue to drive demand for higher spec testing tools. Along with our
forecast of a 75% CAGR for global power supply unit demand in the next

three years (see report), we think this should stay a major driver for Chroma.
• SLT: We are seeing more HPC chip designers adopting the system level
testing process to make sure defects won't be incorporated into the AI
systems. As the first mover in this market. Chroma should benefit from
increasing chip volume as well as higher TDP, in our view.
• Photonics: Transceivers should continue to be the mainstream solution;
architectures such as CPO and NPO also need testing tools.

• Emerging business: We expect Chroma, by leveraging its existing customer
relationship and technology expertise, to gain more traction in FT handlers
and burn-in ovens for AI HPCs in 2027. These are the markets that Chroma
previously focused less on, so they represent new opportunities.
Stay OW: We remain constructive on Chroma for its testing equipment's unique
combination of power, electrical and optical capabilities, allowing it to capture
growth opportunities in various aspects of AI deployment. Our PT remains NT

$2,800 as our estimate increases are offset by a lower target multiple of 40x 2027e
P/E (previously 45x) to factor in generally more conservative sentiment toward the
AI space now. This implies a PEG of 0.6 (vs. 1.0 over the past decade).

A Closer Look
Test instrument and ATS segment set to reach another
record high in 2026

We estimate it will rise 118% YoY to NT$24,564mn (40% of the mix) in 2026. The ever-
increasing power consumption of AI clusters should continue to drive demand for higher
spec testing tools. Along with our forecast of a 75% CAGR for global power supply unit

demand in the coming three years (see our report), this should remain a major driver for
Chroma, in our view.

Multiple drivers in the semiconductor business
System-level testing  (SL T) business
SL T should benefit from the capex cycle of NVIDIA's Rubin generation GPU, AMD's MI450
GPU and more ASIC accelerators.  Management pointed out that discrete testing tools are
still preferred  by major customers.  We are also seeing more HPC chip designers adopting

the system level testing process to make sure defects won't be incorporated into the AI
systems. We believe Chroma, as the first mover in this market, should benefit from
increasing chip volume, as well as higher TDP.
Metrology business
The metrology business should continue to benefit from current advanced packaging
architectures from its foundry and OSAT customers. It is confident that the performance
of its tools can win future architecture projects, which our industry checks suggest should

have higher metrology density.

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
Exhibit 3: Chroma –  Offerings for Metrology in Advanced Packaging
Source: Chroma
Chroma's metrology equipment employs white light interferometry technology. The basic
working principle is that white light is emitted on the reference mirror and the sample,
which are placed at the same distance from the light source. The reflected beams from
the reference mirror and sample surface are then received by a light sensor.
Interference occurs when two light beams collide, which could cause  them to either

strengthen or weaken, depending on the phase of the two light beams. Thus, by
measuring the intensity of the combined light beams, the distance between light sensor
and multiple points on the target surface can be calculated through an algorithm. This can
then be used to measure the dimensions of the structure or the alignment of layers.
Exhibit 4: Concept of Light Interference
Source: Keyence
Exhibit 5: Setup of Interferometer
Source: Wikipedia
CPO contribution to start from 2026

Chroma has been qualified for optical engine testing in Insertion 3 and system-level
testing in Insertion 4, and there might be incremental opportunities if supply chain players'
progress falls behind for testing equipment in Insertion 2. Over the longer term, Chroma
also  aspires to participate in the PIC optical/electrical/reliability testing in Insertion 1.

FT handlers to add another revenue stream
In HPC testing, Chroma used to focus mostly on the SL T side, although it did unveil its first
HPC FT handler earlier this year and is now going through the qualification process. We
believe this is a natural extension of its expertise and customer relationships accumulated
in SL Ts. We expect Chroma to gain more traction in FT handlers for AI HPCs in 2027.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Our price target (base case scenario value) remains  NT$2,800: Our PT is based on a
target multiple of 40x 2027e P/E (vs. 45x 2027e previously).   We lowered our multiple to
factor in recent volatility in market sentiment toward AI.   We view our multiple as justified
based on our estimated 68% operating profit CAGR over 2025-28. This implies a PEG of
0.6 vs. its average of 1.0 in the past decade, when the stock was trading at an average of

~19x forward P/E with an operating profit CAGR of 20%.
Bull case scenario value – NT$3,900 (unchanged): We apply a 55x multiple (vs. 62x P/E
previously)  to our earnings estimate for 2027.  This scenario reflects stronger  5G and HPC
demand, driving more earnings upside.
Bear case scenario value – NT$1,100 (unchanged): We apply a 16x P/E multiple (vs. 18x
P/E previously) to our earnings estimate for 2027. This scenario factors in weaker HPC
revenue as hyperscalers start to focus more on ROI for AI capex than they did in the past

couple of years.
Risks:
• Softer-than-expected AI capex
• Slower progress in advanced packaging capacity expansion
• Weaker consumer electronics and automobile related demand

better captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average
valuation of ~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating proﬁt CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG

project for 2025-28.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 110: 261027_daiwa_TPCA

Source PDF: all report pdf/261027_daiwa_TPCA.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: We attended the 2026 Taiwan Printed Circuit Association (TPCA) Show that 
was held on 20-22 October. Below, we share our read-throughs from booth visits and key 
company takeaways. Overall, we remain positive on Taiwan glass fibre, CCL, PCB, and 
substrate vendors, supported by upbeat company commentary. On pricing, we expect 
low-end CCL and E-glass price increases to be larger and more sustained than previously 
anticipated. High-end CCL vendor commentary suggests plans for more meaningful price 
increases from 2H26 to maintain their products’ relative profitability amid sharp increases 
in low-end CCL prices. We also expect prices for T-glass, ABF, CCL, and substrates to 
continue rising. On the CCL technology roadmap, we favour Q-glass combined with 
hydrocarbon resin (HC) over PTFE-based alternatives as the longer-term direction. Lastly, 
we provide our view on the CCL market competition landscape change after seeing 
Nan Ya Plastics targeting the high-end market more aggressively. We also provide some of 
our key highlights from related companies within the Taiwan PCB supply chain. EMC (2383 
TT, TWD4,750, Buy [1]), ITEQ (6213 TT, TWD746, Buy [1]), Kinsus (3189 TT, TWD941, 
Buy [1]) and NYPCB (8046 TT, TWD1,255, Buy [1]) are our top ideas within our coverage.

We attended the 2026 Taiwan Printed Circuit Association (TPCA) Show last week. Below, 
we share our read-throughs from booth visits and key company takeaways.

Whether to adopt PTFE emerged as a key point of debate at the show.  
• During the TPCA show, Taiwan Union Technology (TUC; 6274 TT, TWD1,280, Buy [1]) 
unveiled its new M10 (Hydrocarbon base) and M11 (PTFE base) materials with glass-
fabric (Low-Dk2, Q-glass) and glass-fabric-free versions. Nan Ya Plastic (1303 TT, not 
rated) also unveiled its M11-grade material (ie, NPG-A99) at the show. Based on our 
understanding, NPG-A99 will continue to a hydrocarbon resin (HC) base, likely 
combined with Low-Dk2, to enhance CCL performance for high frequency and high-
speed signal transmission requirements. However, Elite Material (EMC; 2383 TT, 
TWD4,750, Buy [1]) remains reluctant to enter the PTFE-based CCL market, citing 
limited end application. Also, EMC plans to use advanced Q-glass for its M10 CCL. 
Lastly, we also heard from Panasonic’s booth representative that Panasonic (6752 JP, 
JPY3,687, Neutral [3]; covered by Ayaka Inomata) is pursuing a similar strategy for 
M9/M10 development, leveraging Q-glass instead of PTFE at this stage.

• While Nvidia (NVDA US, USD216, Outperform [2]; covered by Louis Miscioscia) 
appears keen to enhance product performance by adopting PTFE, given its very low 
dissipation factor (Df), PCB manufacturing yields remain a key concern. Accordingly, 
some leading vendors are broadening their material options (including PTFE-based 
CCL) to address potential customer requirements. EMC and Panasonic, however, 
remain firmly committed to a Q-glass-based technology roadmap. Although TUC 
highlighted progress on its PTFE-based M11 at a technology conference during the 
show, we believe it also intends to advance its existing HC resin/Low-Dk2 and HC 
resin/Q-glass roadmap for its next-generation CCL. In our view, its PTFE-based M11 
development is intended to preserve flexibility across potential technology pathways 
rather than replace its existing approach. Nan Ya Plastics’s strategy also appears 
reasonable: it can produce Low-Dk glass in-house but must source Q-glass and PTFE 
externally. This sourcing structure may explain its continued use of Low-Dk2 with HC-
resin instead of PTFE for its next-gen products, including M11.

 Taiwan PCB: Our key takeaways from 2026 TPCA show

Sheng Cheng

Thomas Kim

### Page 2

Taiwan Data Centre Hardware 
27 October 2026 
2

• Conclusion: which technology roadmap will prevail? We believe it is too early to 
identify a clear winner, as the supply chain continues to improve manufacturing yields 
for PTFE-based products. Some vendors remain cautious despite PTFE’s long history in 
the industry, as its thermal shrinkage and relative softness still make adoption in high-
layer-count (HLC) PCBs challenging. Given the current stance of leading global vendors 
EMC, Doosan (000150 KS), and Panasonic, together with EMC’s execution track 
record, we currently favour Q-glass-based solutions without PTFE as the more likely 
direction. Nevertheless, broadening product offerings to address different customer 
requirements should not be viewed negatively, provided vendors have sufficient 
resources to support development across multiple technology pathways.

Low-end CCL and e-glass continue to surprise on the upside.  
• During the show, we also spoke with Kingboard Laminates (1888 HK, not rated). The 
company remained positive on the E-glass outlook, although its booth representative 
provided no numerical guidance for next year following a c.200% price hike in 2026. 
Kingboard attributed the pricing strength to supply constraints, driven largely by AI 
server demand. While we highlighted increased glass yarn supply from Chinese peers in 
2Q/3Q26, the company argued that the weaving machine capacity remains the 
bottleneck. It expects to add only c.500 weaving machines in 2026 and 1.2k in 2027 (vs. 
current installed base of 3.3k). E-glass fabric price increases therefore appear 
supported by limited weaving capacity and producers shifting capacity from lower-end 
glass to higher-end products, including Low-Dk, Low-Dk2, and T-glass. Kingboard also 
noted a similar trend in low-end copper foil, where shortages have recently intensified.

• The read-through from Nan Ya Plastics is similar. The company indicated that its current 
fibreglass allocation between internal consumption and external sales is 45:55, with the 
internal share set to rise gradually as it upgrades its product mix. Its CCL portfolio 
remains concentrated in FR-4 (M2 grade), but it plans to migrate toward M4/M6, 
supported by long-term contract wins with two major hyperscalers seeking to secure 
CCL supply. In 2027, M4/M6 products are expected to account for 60-70% of its mix (4m 
out of 6.5m in capacity), with M2 and below contributing 20%. The remainder would 
comprise high-end CCL, including M7/M8/M9, reflecting recent development progress. 
We therefore expect Nan Ya’s external thin-type E-glass supply to decline as its M4/M6 
mix increases next year.

Competition from Nan Ya Plastics.   
• The current CCL shortage creates favourable conditions for Nan Ya Plastics to expand 
into the high-end CCL supply chain. Whether the company has entered AMZN’s T3/T4 
supply chain is also a key point of market debate. Based on discussions with its booth 
representatives, however, we believe management will prioritise upgrading its M4/M6 
product portfolio before pursuing the highest-end CCL market more aggressively. On the 
positive side, favourable supply-demand dynamics have supported Nan Ya’s transition 
from a low-end supplier toward the mid-to-high-end segment, although high-end CCL 
should still contribute a relatively small share of revenue in 2027. For incumbent 
suppliers, competition is clearly intensifying, but we expect the near-term impact on 
high-end peers to remain limited.

• Our read: We expect the impact on EMC and TUC to remain relatively limited in 2027. 
Competitive pressure could become more meaningful over 2028-2030, although it is too 
early to assess its magnitude.

### Page 3

Taiwan Data Centre Hardware 
27 October 2026 
3

Other key takeaways: 
• Nitto Boseki (3110 JP, JPY2,942, Neutral [3]; covered by Noritsugu Hirakawa) identified 
Taiwan Glass (1802 TT, NR) and Grace Fabric Technology (603256 CH, CNY84.95, 
Outperform [2]; covered by Dennis Ip) as key competitors. Its plan to triple capacity by 
2028 remains largely on track. The company also intends to expand capacity further by 
2030, although the details have yet to be confirmed.

• Resonac (4004 JP, JPY12,915, Outperform [2]; covered by Hidemitsu Umebayashi) 
indicated that customers are requesting ABF substrate core thicknesses of 2,000-
3,000μm, versus its current maximum of 1,600μm, to accommodate larger package 
sizes. Potential technology pathways include adopting glass cores or ceramic 
substrates, or stacking additional core layers to enhance rigidity. Based on discussions 
with booth representatives, substrate makers may move toward multi-core structures, 
which could increase T-glass consumption. However, technical challenges in enabling 
signal transmission between core layers remain a potential barrier that would need to be 
addressed over the next one to two years.

• Mitsui Kinzoku (5706 JP, JPY2,054, Outperform [2]; covered by Shinichiro Ozaki) 
indicated that, based on its technology roadmap, M10-grade CCL combined with its 
HVLP5 copper foil should support 448Gbps SerDes transmission. In the near term, the 
company plans to shift capacity from lower-end to higher-end HVLP products, with a key 
piece of production equipment scheduled for installation in 2028/29. On pricing, its booth 
representative suggested that quarterly increases of more than 10% would be 
appropriate. Regarding copper foil shortages highlighted by other supply-chain 
participants, the representative indicated that mid-tier products, such as HVLP1 and 
HVLP2, could face the most severe supply constraints.

• Unimicron (3037 TT, TWD1,060, Buy [1]) appeared to highlight multi-core structures and 
the embedding of passive components within core layers as key technology trends in 
ABF substrates.

• Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT; 4958 TT, not rated) indicated that its substrate business 
has a plan to list on the Hong Kong Stock Exchange in 1H27 under the name Leading 
Interconnect Semiconductor Technology (LIST). At the show, the company focused 
primarily on thick HDI boards and highlighted two next-generation technology trends: 1) 
sequential lamination (M+N) to improve signal integrity and increase routing density 
within the same footprint; and 2) hybrid HDI/HLC structures, which add HDI layers to 
thick HLC boards (M+N), as discussed earlier.

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 6,200 TWD | Price: 3,915 TWD

3Q26 beat on consumer builds; 4Q26 lifted by TPU ramp 
3Q26 sales were +10% QoQ on its flagship mobile SoC launch and customers’ pre-builds 
ahead of its chipset price hike on rising manufacturing cost. On higher sales and better 
mix, GM/OpM edged up to 46%/16%, taking 3Q26 EPS to NT$17.5 above BofAe/street. 
4Q26 sales was guided +25-32% QoQ, in line with BofA preview, as steep TPUv8t ramp 
now tracking to US$2.5bn (vs. US$2bn guide before) partially balanced by soft demand 
elsewhere (-15-22% QoQ vs. 5-year seasonal +1%) following the early pull-in. Despite 
TPUv8t growing to ~35% of its sales at lower margins, GMs is guided resiliently at 45% 
in 4Q26, implying better margins for its merchant business at 47%. Fast sales scale-up 
and stable GMs should lift operating leverage with OpMs guided for 19% at midpoint. 
ASIC pipeline spanning across CSPs and frontier AI labs 
Mediatek sees TPUv8t ramping to US$16bn+ revenue in ’27, lifting OpM to 20%+, with 
upside if it could secure more capacity. The follow on TPUv9 is tracking for a meaningful 
production potential in ’28. Importantly, it confirmed a 2nd ASIC customer win in design 
now and production contribution set to kick off in ’28. We believe this new project would 
require Mediatek’s component sourcing and supply chain management at a much higher 
dollar content. This along with potential Bytedance win could add upside to our base 
case for its data center revenue of US$19bn/US$53bn in '27/'28 with xAI ASIC revenue 
joined from 2H28 to supplement Google TPU upgrades.  
AI project diversification supports earnings and valuation 
As ASIC business inflects, we lift our 2026 EPS to NT$74.13 and keep ‘27/28 EPS at 
NT$168.93/404.32. Keep our Buy rating and NT$6,200 PO on 24x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(implied 30x ASIC EPS + 10x merchant EPS), backed by sales/EPS 90%/134% CAGR. 
Growing AI project wins should alleviate the concerns on customer concentration and 
TPU competition debate with share downside protected by 23x/10x ‘27/28 straight P/E.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
MediaTek is a fabless IC design company supplying to 
wireless communications and digital consumer markets. 
Before taking leadership in mobile SoC area, it had built 
solid ground in the TV SoC and optical disk drive markets. 
The firm invests heavily in R&D to seek better positioning 
in the 5G cycle, and has a complete offering including ASIC, 
Wi-Fi, PMIC etc. to address applications beyond 
smartphone, such as IoT and auto.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on MediaTek as we think MediaTek is 
ready and has the strength to brace for the upcoming 
proliferation of IoT devices and opportunities in growing 
markets like ASIC, ARM-based PC, auto, and etc., and we 
believe its solid balance sheet and cashflow could provide 
downside support to the share price. Meanwhile, we expect 
5G smartphone penetration to be a multi-year theme for 
the company, with its better competitive landscape in 
duopoly 5G era and an improved margin profile.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
MediaTek (MDTKF) 
Our PO of NT$6,200 is based on 24x 2H27-1H28 P/E. which is towards the higher end 
of its historical P/E range of 8-25x, as we believe the company's broadening ASIC 
pipeline could drive an earnings upgrade cycle.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) better than expected smartphone demand, especially in 
China, (2) a better pricing in mobile communication segment thanks to alleviated 
competition and rising exposure to high-end/flagship market, and (3) an even more 
superior investment return on non-op side to uplift its net cash position. (4) Unexpected 
collaboration with global technology leaders leads to higher than expected 
margins/profits

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) weaker-than-expected end-market situation in 
consumer-related smartphone/TV space, (2) macro challenges including inflation/policy-
related pressures, (3) rising competition from existing players and/or newcomers in 
smartphone/TV SoC (system on chip) space, and (4) ASP/margin pressure caused by each 
or all of the above and/or rising foundry costs. (4) Bidding process for Meta Inference 
ASIC is slower than expected.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating.  VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation). 
Investment rating Total return expectation (within 12-month period of date of initial 
rating)

INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be 
secure) and 9 - pays no cash dividend.  Coverage Cluster is comprised of stocks covered by a single analyst or two or more analysts sharing a c ommon industry, 
sector, region or other classification(s). A stock’ s coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.

Earnings revision: We factor in Landmark 3Q26 result and revise up earnings by 9%/ 
3%/ 2%/ 1% in 2026-29E mainly on higher revenues of SiPh CW lasers, supported by 
strong client demand and capacity expansion. We revise up GM by 1ppts in 2026E on 
mix upgrade, and revise down Opex ratio in 2026-29E to reflect improving operational 
efficiency amid the larger scale.

Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology (unchanged) and apply a 61.5x 
(unchanged, report link) target P/E multiple on our 2029E EPS, discounted back to 
2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, risk free rate 1.6%, and market risk premium at 
5.1% - unchanged). Our target P/E multiple of 61.5x is derived from the PEG&M ratio of 
peers (unchanged) in the photonics supply chain. Consequently, our 12-month target 
price is now NT$4,490 (vs. previously at NT$4,459). Our new TP implied 2028E P/E of 
75x is between company’s avg. P/E at 62x and avg. +1 P/E at 91x. Maintain Buy.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - LandMark 
Price Target Risks and Methodology - LandMark Optoelectronics Corp.  
Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology, applying a 61.5x target P/E 
multiple on our 2029E EPS, then discount it back to 2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, 
risk free rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), to derive our 12-month target 
price of NT$4,490. Our target P/E multiple is based on the same PEG&M ratio as peers in 
the photonics supply chain. We are Buy-rated on LandMark. 
Key risk: Slower-than-expected SiPh penetration; stronger-than-expected competition; 
Integrated Device Manufacturers (IDMs) offering Epitaxial (EPI) wafers in-house.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

한화에어로스페이스 (012450)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 방위산업 | 2026. 11. 2  
방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감

방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감 
지상방산 매출 1조 2,450억원으로 전년비 41%, 영업이익 217억원으로 전년비 
96.2% 급감하며 영업이익률은 1.7% 기록. 실적 급감에 대해 사측은 올해 주요 사업
의 납품 일정이 4분기에 집중됨에 따라 3분기 인도 물량이 적었던 것과 대전 사업장 
생산 중단에 따른 영향 때문이라고 설명. 단, 4분기에는 당초 계획에 따라 주요 사업의 
납품 물량이 집중되어 있어 실적이 개선될 것이고 대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 내에 정
상 가돌 하는 것이 목표라고 밝힘 
수주잔고 증가세 지속으로 성장 지속 가능할 것 
3분기 말 수주잔고 35조원으로 전년비 12.9% 증가. 이를 통한 지상방산 부문 성장 
지속 가능할 것으로 판단. 참고로 사측은 내년 매출 15% 수준 성장 전망 유지. 하지
만 금리 상승에 따른 글로벌 업종 평균 밸류에이션 하락에 따른 방산 부문 적용 멀티
플 하향 조정 그리고 상장 연결회사(한화오션, 한화시스템, 쎄트렉아이)와 상장 비연결
회사(한국항공우주)의 주가 하락 반영 영향으로 목표주가는 하향 조정 
8월 미 육군 차륜형 자주포 사업에서 시제 사업자로 선정. 향후 K9MH는 미 육군의 
작전 운용 개념에 맞춰 맞춤 조정을 거친 후에 특별한 결함 혹은 결격 사유가 없으면 
최종 계약 체결 예정. 동시에 미 육군의 궤도형 자주포 사업에도 참여할 계획. 뿐만 아
니라 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등으로 신시장 개척 역시 지속되고 있다는 점도 긍
정적 
 Analyst 이재광 
rheejaekwang @lssec.co.kr

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
We reaffirm our expectation  that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board will again increase the cash rate at the November
meeting. Today’s inflation data and Senate testimony are
the final pieces of information before the RBA Monetary
Policy Board (MPB) meets next week. Recent data and

communication have consistently pointed to the staff
recommending an increase in the cash rate. We expect a
majority of the external members to agree, though some
members might dissent.
Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions. It

will therefore deem that further policy tightening is needed.
The RBA has made it clear that it is down-weighting the recent
easing in the labour market, even though this was more than it
forecast. In today’s opening statement  to the Senate hearing,
the Governor said, “Despite the unemployment rate recently
rising a little more than we had expected, the labour market
seems a little tighter than we had previously judged. The
evidence for this is elevated underlying inflationary pressures.

That is, when we exclude some of the large volatile price
movements such as fuel we see inflationary pressure in areas
that reflect domestic demand for goods and services.” In the
Senate, the Governor also said that they were not worried
about the current pace of increase in unemployment.
Of course, this takes no account of the lagged relationship
between the labour market and inflation. The argument is that
if that measure of inflation last quarter was too high, it must

therefore be that the labour market is too tight. Nor does it
allow sufficiently for non-labour cost shocks, which business
surveys indicate are the real issue. But whether this judgement
by the RBA turns out to be right or not will not be revealed
for some time, certainly not soon enough to change its mind
before next week. And while some MPB members might have
a more optimistic view about labour supply trends than the
RBA staff, they are likely to be a minority.

The RBA is also relatively sanguine about the current
downturn in the housing market. The Governor and other
RBA officials are at pains to highlight that they do not target
housing prices; nor should they. The RBA considers that most,
though not all, of the current downswing is an expected effect
of tighter monetary policy.
RBA to hike in November, as inflation remains too high
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The question then turns to the future beyond November. While

we still expect a hawkish tone in the RBA’s communication
until inflation has clearly turned down, further hikes beyond
next week are not currently our base case. Clearly, though, the
risks of another hike – most likely in February – have increased.
Our current forecast for Q4 inflation would seem to give them
cover for such a move. We note, however, that home-building
costs in the CPI have undershot expectations in the past couple
of months. This might imply a different view of the extent of

domestic demand pressures than the RBA currently believes.
However, a lot can happen in three months, as we have just
seen. The conflict in the Middle East  could escalate further,
lifting energy prices beyond our current base case. It could
also go the other way, though, if the US and Iran manage to
strike a post-midterm-election deal. And as their counterparts
in New Zealand highlighted today, one risk is that the global
economy weakens suddenly. In the post-meeting statement ,

the RBNZ Monetary Policy Committee said, “the world
economy could tip abruptly into a downturn causing weaker
inflation over the medium term.” This risk was something the
RBNZ MPC thought could crystallise in the next six weeks.
Whether on this timetable or something more drawn out, this
possibility does not appear to be on the RBA’s radar. Nor is it
our base case, though we have previously worried that the AI
financing boom could hit a wall quite suddenly.

“We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA
Monetary Policy Board will again increase the
cash rate at the November meeting"

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related
risks through 2027; although, in the absence of another shock,
we expect them to remain on hold.
The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view

on the breadth of growth, the 3.4% annualised gain for GDP
benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household
consumption, 12.7% growth in non-residential investment
as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment
was effectively offset by net exports’ drag given most of
the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is
imported. This is not to say that US firms won’t benefit from

the investment once complete, but simply that much of the
initial return goes to the foreign businesses who provide the
necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks
remain for the consumer, the increase in nominal spending
seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis,
consumption flat in the month following a 0.5% gain in
August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the
outlook is expected to see household consumption growth

average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE
inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr medium-term target as
the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline
and core measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy
rates unchanged in October. Of note from the meeting
communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks

for inflation, but also downside risks for growth. "While the
full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying
inflation has remained broadly stable so far and wages do not
show a material response". "Financing conditions have [also]
tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth".
In the press conference, President Lagarde noted that,
between the October and December meetings, a significant
amount of new economic information will be released,

allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.
Based on the October meeting communications, we continue
to expect a rate hike at the December meeting but believe this
will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the
spectrum of risks Europe faces is broad and skewed towards
inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that
she will step down as ECB President in February ahead of the

scheduled conclusion of her term next October.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI .
Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in the month, largely
owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in
domestic and international holiday travel & accommodation.
The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than
anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).
While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation –
housing in particular looks to be cooling faster than expected

as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are
still showing persistence. Highlighting this is market services
ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator
of price pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this
metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail points
to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for
another chunky read in Q4.
With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts,

and some of the energy risks flagged then materialising, we
continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp
rate hike to 4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate
hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market
expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding instead to wait for
further information before taking the next step with policy. The
decision was made 4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A

25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening in 2027
(Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems
probable; however, each decision will depend on fuel prices,
inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady  at its October
meeting. Based on prior comments by key members, the
decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook

and Cleveland Fed President Hammack’s dissents in favour
of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at
a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Chair
Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic
growth outside of tech-related investment (evinced by Q3
GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in
the statement. However, inflation “remains elevated” and the

FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable
return of annual inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will
help the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests
another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter, the FOMC
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
A lesson to be learned
The RBNZ delivered the biggest shock to markets outside of
a crisis period in over 20 years this week by leaving the OCR
unchanged. Markets had priced around a 98% probability of
a 25bp increase, and all major domestic analysts concurred.

After the CPI data the week earlier, we belatedly shifted our
call to a hike despite plenty of good arguments in favour of
the RBNZ sticking to its plan to pause. With the benefit of
hindsight, too much weight was put on market pricing and
short-term inflation drivers and not enough on the medium
term. That’s an important lesson to be learned.
The highlight of an action-packed week of central banking news
was the unexpected decision of the RBNZ to leave the OCR at

2.75%, despite near unanimous analyst expectations and market
pricing in favour of a 25bp hike. We had shifted sides from a
hold to a hike just a week earlier, in the wake of a CPI outcome
that was weaker than RBNZ expectations on the surface, but
with some evidence of firm core inflation undertones.
While we clearly cannot claim to have gotten this call right,
we always thought that the outcome was not the “done deal”
that markets had priced in. For example, in our preview of the

meeting we noted:
“We still think a more measured tightening approach is
appropriate from here. Raising the OCR at every meeting
until something ‘breaks’ could be a risky strategy if downside
growth concerns crystalise.”
And we saw a vote on the decision as very possible:
“A vote seems very possible as differences in view will likely
remain among the MPC about how much the medium-term
inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.”
The 4:3 vote that was ultimately required to bring the MPC

to a decision is a testament to just how finely balanced the
decision was. We had thought that the hike was perhaps
around a 60-70% probability whereas the Record of Meeting
implies something more like a 50:50 call.
In the final analysis, the RBNZ has merely followed through on
the pause it signalled for this meeting back in the September
Monetary Policy Statement. All MPC members continue to see
a case for a higher OCR in time, but there are different views

around the timing of those increases. Governor Breman noted
that further interest rate increases remain likely in coming
meetings but are conditional on the whether the outlook
evolves in line with expectations. Indeed, the Governor noted
in later comments to press that a December hike was a “high”
probability and that the outlook remains for OCR “hikes” as the
base case. This suggests that the RBNZ’s view on the terminal
OCR hasn’t really shifted down from the 3.25% level presented

back in September. And it may be reasonable to speculate,
subject to data evolving as the RBNZ expects, that a modest
upgrade in those forward OCR forecasts could be in the offing.
So how did we all get this so wrong? This outcome was a real
shock given the historical record shows that over the history of
the OCR the market has without exception correctly predicted
a 25bp hike when the implied probability built into markets
was as high as it was. Clearly some recalibration of the model

is required. We can identify two key areas where lessons
should be learned: too much weight on market pricing; and not
enough on drivers of medium-term inflation.
Market pricing did not adequately reflect the
MPC’s desire to shift strategy .
Firstly, and most importantly, we put too much weight on
market pricing and too little on the RBNZ’s clearly stated
strategy of preferring a pause at the October meeting to
assess the impact of interest rate increases already done. In our

Preview we incorrectly surmised that silence equated to assent
and that market pricing of a hike would make an OCR hike the
less disruptive option – especially given the sense that a hike
to 3% was still likely in December as implied by the RBNZ’s
September forecasts. From a macroeconomic perspective,
raising rates six weeks early is of little consequence.
This logic, as sound as it was, was wrong, as market pricing did
not reflect a large enough change in the medium-term inflation

outlook to justify a change in RBNZ strategy. Markets at least
partially held the view, based on a long history of revealed
RBNZ preference, that the RBNZ keeps moving the OCR in the
same direction until confronted with compelling evidence that
a halt to the cycle is in prospect. That operating approach may
be under review now.
It’s not uncommon for other central banks to take breaks
while amid an interest rate cycle to gather information on
the impact of work already done before moving forward.

This week’s pause by the FOMC in the face of a fierce set of
monthly inflation readings in the last month is an example.
Pausing does not need to imply stopping or reversing. Indeed,
taking a more measured approach could prove to be solid
risk management – as we identified in our MPR preview note.
Risk management concerns probably should have greater
prominence in this cycle given we are seeing a measure of
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
supply side cost shocks that might not be persistent. The
Statement of Record contains a wealth of material related to
these sorts of uncertainties and concerns. Markets were not
listening to these concerns, however.
An insufficient focus on drivers of medium-term

inflation
Markets were not listening to the alarm bells because there
was a heavy focus on factors that may be more relevant to the
short-term inflation outlook than medium-term inflation. Top
of mind were the rise in energy prices and fall in the exchange
rate. Both factors look set to significantly boost the short-
term inflation outlook – for example we currently forecast
December quarter annual inflation of 4.3%, with upside risks
from there if fuel prices do not fall over the balance of the

quarter. However, our analysis of the data surprises suggested
no significant shift to the implied RBNZ OCR track one year
ahead. Upward pressure on the future OCR projection from
the lower exchange rate and stronger GDP was balanced
by higher long term interest rates and the lower Q3 CPI
outcome. Higher energy prices were not seen as a key factor
as they don’t impact medium term inflation unless inflation
expectations are dislodged (no evidence was found of that

since September) or if the increase in energy prices is viewed
as persistent (futures markets continue to imply lower forward
energy prices, although MPC hawks clearly see risks of
persistent inflation coming from supply shocks).
The RBNZ’s communications at the MPR and in its aftermath
are directing markets to pay more attention to medium-term
inflation drivers in the future. Hence the references to the
strength of demand and inflation expectations. The risk to

the RBNZ’s inflation forecasts are to the upside, and hence
we might reasonably expect some upgrade of the OCR profile
when we see new forecasts in December. But it’s not a done
deal by any means as data and events between now and then
will dictate what stance the RBNZ takes. This week’s business
and consumer confidence reports do not seem to present many
smoking guns of imminent second-round inflation pressures,
even though business and consumer sentiment seems to be

taking the higher oil price in its stride.
Is core inflation high enough yet to shift the
strategy?
Another interesting point made by the Governor was that the
markets (and analysts) misinterpreted how the September
quarter CPI was read by the RBNZ, which evidently took
greater comfort from the outcome. Governor Breman has noted
she wished the RBNZ has protocols in place to communicate
with markets the RBNZ’s view on that CPI data given that it
seems to have been a factor that figured importantly in at least

some MPC member’s thinking. Headline inflation was lower
than the RBNZ expected and importantly the core inflation
measures, that we had thought might have been of concern to
the MPC, do not yet seem to have been at a level sufficient to
shift the RBNZ from its pause-then-hike strategy. We remain
sceptical that a 3.25% OCR will prove sufficient to bring core
inflation down significantly. But core inflation appears to need
to lift for the MPC to become more agitated. It likely will lift,

but we won’t get more data on that until January 2027.
December hike likely but not a done deal
We expect a further 25bp hike at this year’s final meeting on 9
December and then 25bp increases in the February and March
2027 meetings, followed by a slower pace of increases at the
June and September Monetary Policy Statement meetings.
Our forecast of a peak OCR of 4% from September 2027 has
remained unchanged throughout, as has our assessment of a
3.75% neutral OCR.

That said, in the current uncertain environment, it goes without
saying that the evolution of monetary policy will depend on
how both global events and key data evolve. Therefore, as the
RBNZ noted itself, the exact timing of the tightening profile
is highly uncertain and even the tightening we forecast at the
December meeting should not be regarded as a done deal.
Looking ahead, the ‘hardest’ data to come will be the Q3
labour market surveys where we anticipate a small fall in

the unemployment rate to 5.5% but still moderate wage
pressures. The December quarter RBNZ inflation expectations
surveys will be of significant interest (17, 20 and 24
November) for MPC members looking for persistent inflation
impacts. The usual run of monthly activity and selected prices
indicators will be useful in narrowing down Q4 growth and
inflation momentum. And finally, the MPC’s focus on external
risks will imply keeping an eye on global fixed income, equity

and credit markets for signs of imminent calamity.
Finally, November 7 is the general election. A close race is
envisaged that could impact on the RBNZ’s mandate. We
will be offering client briefings on Monday 9 November and
Tuesday 10 November to outline the immediate implications
of the election outcome for markets and the economy. Contact
your relationship manager for details.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: RBA Policy Decision (%)
Nov 3, Last: 4.60, Westpac f/c: 4.85
Market f/c: 4.85, Range: 4.60 to 4.85
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) will decide to raise the cash rate by 25bps to
4.85% at the November meeting.

Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions.
Recent data and communication have consistently pointed to
the RBA staff recommending an increase in the cash rate. We
expect a majority of the external board members to agree,

though some members might dissent.
AUS: Oct Cotality Home Value Index (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –1.1, Westpac f/c: –1.3
The Cotality home value index – covering all of Australia – fell
1.1% in Sep with downward revisions shaving a further 0.4%
off prices. The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2%
from their March peak, annual growth stalling flat. Cotality
reported 97% of capital city suburbs experienced falls over the
three months to September.
The October update is set to be another weak one with an

added hit coming from the RBA’s interest rate rise in late
September. The daily measure (covering the five major capital
cities rather than all of Australia) is tracking a 1.5% decline vs
1.3% in September. The detail points to slightly bigger falls
in Sydney, Brisbane and Perth with Melbourne continuing
to record more moderate declines. Note that seasonality is
usually a slight positive in the October month.
AUS: Sep Dwelling Approvals (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –6.1, Westpac f/c: –7.0

Market f/c: 2.0, Range: –7.0 to 5.0
Dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in August, with the annual
pace easing slightly to 10.3%yr. Weakness centred on unit
approvals with private detached house approvals actually
posting a solid rebound from a July month fall, touching a new
cycle high (up 18.4%yr).
We expect the September update to show a much clearer
weakening. New construction is coming under pressure
from multiple fronts: interest rate rises; a sharp downturn in

established markets; and rising costs – with a great deal of
uncertainty around each of these. New home sales have fell
sharply through the September quarter and we expect to
see detached house approvals show something similar. Unit
approvals are coming off a weak read and should be more
stable. Total approvals are expected to print 7% lower.

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Goods Trade Balance ($bn)
Nov 5, Last: 0.5, Westpac f/c: 2.0
Market f/c: 1.0, Range: –1.0 to 2.5
Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August.
Recently, the trade balance has been oscillating between
small deficits and surpluses with significant volatility in the

underlying trade mix.
Swings in non-monetary gold, fuel and cars have all played
a large role, but the most eye-catching result from Aug was
the 79% surge in automatic data processing equipment (ADP)
imports, which captures the investment upswing related to
data centres.
For September, we expect an increase in the surplus as the
ADP import surge unwinds, though we are likely in store for
ongoing monthly volatility in trade for some time.
NZ: Sep Building Consents (%mth)

Nov 3, Last: +5.6%, Westpac f/c: Flat
Annual residential building consent numbers are currently at
a three year high, and we expect that they will remain firm in
the September update. However, monthly consent numbers
are showing signs of peaking, with falls in three of the last four
months. We’re watching to see if that trend has continued,
particularly given the ongoing softness in the housing
market.  In the non-residential space, we’ve seen softness

in the number of retail and office developments, but greater
resilience in the industrial and storage segment.
AUS: Sep Household Spending Indicator (%mth)
Nov 4, Last: flat, Westpac f/c: –0.5
Market f/c: 0.2, Range: –0.5 to 0.8
The household spending indicator was unchanged in August,
annual growth easing to 6.8%yr. The detail showed a modest
rotation away from discretionary categories towards non-
discretionary.
Our Westpac-DataX Card Tracker  showed a decent rise in

the September month although new vehicle sales (which are
not covered by the tracker but are in scope for the spending
indicator) look to have posted a decline. Overall we expect
the indicator to show a 0.5% decline in the September month.
Note that the update with also include Q3 estimates of real
spending – we expect this to show a 0.6%qtr gain with prices
up 1.2%qtr and nominal spend up 1.8%qtr.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 Labour Market Surveys (%)
Nov 4, Unemployment Rate, Last: 5.6, Westpac f/c: 5.5
LCI Private Sector, Last: 0.7, Westpac f/c: 0.5
We expect the unemployment rate to edge down to 5.5%
for the September quarter. Job numbers have been gaining
momentum in recent months, and now look to be enough to

outstrip population growth. However, the existing slack in the
labour market is likely to be keeping wage growth subdued,
despite cost-of-living pressures on workers. Our forecasts are
a little more positive overall than what the RBNZ assumed in
its September MPS forecasts.
US: Oct Employment Report
Nov 6, Payrolls, Last: 29k, Market f/c: 55k, WBC f/c: 75k
Nov 6, U/E rate, Last: 4.2%, Market f/c: 4.2%, WBC f/c: 4.3%
Nonfarm payrolls have shown considerable volatility of late,

July’s -10k followed by a 133k gain in August then a 29k
increase in September. However, the 3-month average of 51k
is likely a good guide on underlying momentum. In October, we
look for a 75k gain, although we expect some of that increase
to be offset by a modest reduction to the prior two months,
keeping the 3-month average around 50k.
While the participation rate has lifted in recent months, it
remains well below the average of 2025. Month-to-month, a

change either way is not guaranteed, but it seems likely that
the participation rate will trend higher in coming months. We
therefore expect the unemployment rate to edge up.

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025.

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T 
 — PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ.

Phương án bổ sung vốn và tái cơ cấu hoạt động kinh doanh 
— Đại hội thông qua phương án chào bán tối đa 550 triệu cổ phiếu. Nguồn vốn dự kiến 
phân bổ 60% cho kế hoạch kinh doanh gắn với điều chỉnh chính sách thu đổi, 20% bổ 
sung vốn lưu động và phần còn lại bổ sung vốn lưu động và/hoặc trả vay ngắn hạn. 
Nhu cầu vốn Q4/2026 và Q1/2027 lần lượt khoảng 3,100 tỷ VND và 2,800 tỷ VND. Giá và 
khối lượng phát hành thực tế chưa được xác định tại đại hội.  
— Doanh nghiệp dự kiến đóng 25–30 cửa hàng và mở khoảng 10 cửa hàng (có chọn lọc) 
trong năm 2026, tương ứng giảm ròng 15–20 cửa hàng; đồng thời đặt mục tiêu giảm 
20–30% chi phí nhân sự. Cổ tức tiền mặt năm 2026 được điều chỉnh về 0%; cổ tức 2025 
về 10% mệnh giá và đã chi trả. 
— 100% mẫu kim cương được AIGS và Vinacontrol kiểm định độc lập là kim cương thiên 
nhiên, phù hợp thông số 4C. PNJ dự kiến trang bị máy kiểm định tại các cửa hàng trọng 
điểm để khách hàng kiểm tra trực tiếp.

Đánh giá của KBSV 
— KBSV cho rằng huy động vốn từ phát hành riêng lẻ là phương án phù hợp ở thời điểm 
hiện tại trong bối cảnh lãi suất tăng cao, giúp PNJ cân đối thanh khoản và duy trì hoạt 
động liên tục. Tuy nhiên, với khối lượng phát hành lớn và chưa có phương án chi tiết, 
doanh nghiệp sẽ cần thêm thời gian để hoàn thành phương án phát hành và cân đối 
giữa lợi ích của cổ đông và doanh nghiệp.  
— Nhu cầu yếu tại Trung Quốc và cạnh tranh từ kim cương nuôi cấy giá thấp tiếp tục gây 
áp lực lên giá kim cương tự nhiên, khiến PNJ gặp khó khăn trong việc tiêu thụ tồn kho 
nhóm kim cương  và gia tăng tổn thất khi mua lại theo chính sách cũ. Thực tế, tỷ trọng 
kim cương và trang sức kim cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% cuối Q2/2026 lên 
52% cuối Q3/2026, với giá trị ước khoảng 9,000 tỷ VND. KBSV cho rằng cần theo dõi (1) 
nhu cầu thị trường trong giai đoạn cuối năm; (2) tốc độ giảm tồn kho và dòng tiền thu 
về từ bán hàng; và (3) giá trị khách hàng bán lại. Diễn biến các yếu tố này sẽ quyết định 
khả năng thu hồi vốn, mức độ phục hồi biên lợi nhuận và nhu cầu trích lập bổ sung 
trong quá trình xử lý. 
— Ước tính bán niên sử dụng tỷ lệ tổn thất 20.3% đối với hàng bán trong giai đoạn 2020–
2026 dự kiến được thu mua lại, thấp hơn mức 28.9% trên các giao dịch mua lại thực tế 
từ 01/07–03/09/2026. Vì vậy, bên cạnh giả định thu đổi 80%, mức tổn thất khi xử lý 
hàng mua lại là yếu tố quan trọng cần theo dõi. Việc chuyển hạch toán dự phòng từ 
SG&A sang giá vốn tại kỳ soát xét bán niên cũng cho thấy áp lực biên lợi nhuận bị co 
hẹp trong quá trình tái cấu trúc về mặt kế toán.

## Document 364: First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-11-03)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-11-03).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change
of OCR call
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:19 October 22 2026 Share
Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter and 3.7% y/y. Core inflation
pressures are elevated and market pricing is primed for a 25bp OCR hike next week, which
we now expect.
Consumers Price Index, September quarter 2026 – key stats

### Page 2

Headline inflation
Quarterly change: +0.7% (prev: +1.5%)
Westpac forecast: +0.7%
RBNZ: +0.8%
Market median: +0.8%, range +0.5% to +1.2%

Annual change: +3.7% (prev: +4.1%)
Westpac forecast: +3.7%, RBNZ: +3.9%, Market: +3.8%

Non-tradables
Quarterly change: +1.0% (prev: +0.6%)
Westpac forecast: +1.3%
Annual change: +3.3% (prev: +3.4%)

Tradables
Quarterly change: +0.2% (prev: +2.7%)
Westpac forecast: -0.2%
Annual change: +4.3% (prev: +4.9%)

Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter. That saw the annual inflation rate easing to
3.7%, down from 4.1% in the year to June. 
Today’s result was in line with our forecast, but lower than the RBNZ’s forecast for 3.9% annual
inflation. 
However, under the surface, the details were more concerning. Measure of core inflation have taken a
step higher, with several closely watched measures now at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s
target band. 
We suspect that today’s result will leave the market continuing to price a strong chance of another
OCR hike at next Wednesday’s policy review. In that light, we think the RBNZ will regard this as the
path of least resistance, and we now expect that they will deliver (previously we expected that they
would pause and assess, before hiking at the December MPS).

What contributed to inflation in the September quarter?
Underpinning September’s 0.7% rise in consumer prices were some big price movements in both
directions. 
The largest move in prices this quarter was actually a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by
any stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for both petrol and diesel over the September
quarter were lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle East

### Page 3

conflict. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91 unleaded was 6.5% lower in September than in
the June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower. Those moves took 0.4ppts off inflation
this quarter. 
While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully
high. And that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other goods and services. Of note, the
September quarter saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with international airfares up 14%
and domestic airfares up nearly 8%. 
The September quarter also saw the annual increases in local council rates (up 6.2%) and charges for
services like water supply (up 7.5%). That continues the run of large increases seen in recent years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices, largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
prices for fresh vegetables. 
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in housing rents, which are the largest component of
the CPI, accounting for 11.2% of the basket. With low population growth and ample supply, rents were
flat in the September quarter and have effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual terms,
rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Annual and core inflation 
The annual inflation rate dropped back to 3.7% in the September quarter, down from 4.1% in the year
to June and the highest level in two years.
Looking under the surface, prices in the domestically oriented non-tradables group rose 1.0% over the
quarter, less than expected. That saw annual non-tradables inflation nudging down to 3.3% (from 3.4%
in the year to June). 
Non-tradables inflation has continued to run at above-average rates over the past year. That’s despite
the softness in economic growth and the labour market. In large part, that’s due to the continued
sizeable increases in administered prices, such as electricity charges which rose 9.3% over the past
year. 
However, prices in other areas have also been firm. Non-tradables excluding housing and utilities costs
(which also omits local council rates) was 3.4% for the year. In seasonally adjusted terms, quarterly
non-tradables inflation has been tracking around 0.8% for most of the past year. It was noted that in
sectors like construction, higher materials costs are adding to prices. Feedback from business we've
heard indicates that those higher materials costs are likely related to high oil prices. 
On the imported front, lower fuel costs saw tradable prices rising by just 0.2% over the September
quarter (though above our forecast for a small fall). That saw annual tradables inflation dropping back
to 4.3%, down sharply from the 4.9% rise in the year to June. 
While fuel costs have been a key driver for tradables inflation, under the surface, other imported
inflation pressures are looking firm. Excluding food and fuel costs, tradables prices were up 2.7% over
the past year. That’s the highest level in three years. 
With big swings in fuel costs, and concern that this is spilling over into operating costs more generally,
a key focus for the RBNZ is what’s been happening to measures of core inflation (which smooth
through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual prices and instead track the underlying trend in

### Page 4

Related articles
inflation). 
On this front we did some see worrying signs. Several closely watched measures of core inflation have
picked up, and now sit at the top of (or slightly above) the RBNZ target band. In terms of specifics, we
saw: 
Reinforcing this picture of firm underlying inflation pressures, a growing number of goods have been
increasing in price. That could be an early indication that businesses, even those with a limited
exposure to fuel costs, are starting to factor in expectations of continued high inflation when setting
prices.

Outlook 
While today’s result was lower than the RBNZ assumed in their September MPS projections, the
firmness in core inflation will give the RBNZ pause for thought ahead of their policy review on 28
October. Furthermore, with a sharp rise in petrol prices in recent weeks, September’s easing in annual
inflation is likely to be just a temporary reprieve. We expect headline inflation to reaccelerate back over
4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain above the RBNZ’s current forecast for most of
2027. 
Market pricing and views have coalesced around an October hike which we now suspect the MPC will
see as the path of least resistance. Core inflation measures are now close to or above 3%, which will
add fuel to arguments from the MPC hawks. We will have more detail in our MPR Preview later today,
including on the implications for the shape of the tightening cycle moving forward.

CPI ex-fuel and food: 3.2% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
CPI ex-fuel, food and household energy: +2.9% yr (vs +2.5% previously)
30% trimmed mean: +3.0% (vs +2.6% previously)
Weighted median: +3.1% (vs +2.7% previously)

## Document 376: How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 7
